If Members sitting on the Health Committee were to support my recent proposal to delete Article 78a of the Health and Health Insurance Act, they would not. Nor are the Members of both parties who carry the word'social' in the title. This article abolishes the right to treatment, except for emergency, self-employed entrepreneurs, their family members and their children who are insured by them, even though they are not able to pay health care contributions for justified reasons. Thus, every day around 8 000 citizens, including children, pregnant women and the elderly, remain without the right to receive treatment from public funds, except for emergency treatment. In Slovenia, access to treatment from public funds is denied to many others, the poorest groups of the population. Among them are many older people, people with low pensions and salaries, and single women who cannot afford supplementary insurance. Instead of a system of health and related social security measures, a member of the Health Committee offered to treat the poor in voluntary clinics, charity that we had given up universal health insurance years ago. I wonder who in this country has given up universal access to treatment. Most citizens certainly don't! I therefore hope that, in the future, there will also be a requirement for universal health care in the report of the Ombudsman between the amendments and the end of Article 78a of the Health and Health Insurance Act. I'll back up the report. Thank you very much.

I am convinced that since such acquired property is illegal at the time of its acquisition, it is not a violation of the alleged innocence or a violation of the constitutional right to property. In my view, the implementation of the law should not lead to further legal complications and even a possible remedy due to alleged unconstitutionality. As a proposal, I would like to point out that this is a proposal for a law that will make an important contribution to strengthening the awareness that Slovenia is a rule of law and that it will prevent any abuse in this area. I therefore expect the National Assembly to adopt it at this meeting and before the completion of its term of office. I believe that Members should no longer afford inter-party accounting or electoral populism at the time of their consideration. We must follow the objective that no one can keep his property if he has acquired it in an illegal way or through illegal activity. This objective can only be achieved if the law is enforceable and effective. I would therefore like to draw attention to the fact that we have already made its implementation or effectiveness difficult on the Committee by adopting the Supplement to Article 57, which extended the application of the Act throughout the period since 1989 and since the beginning of 1990. This is a criminal offence for which a penalty of five years or more may be imposed. Therefore, we will not support this amendment, nor will I support the other amendments of the SDS Group, by which they wish to reduce by law the time and the rest, due to the difficulties that the proposed solutions would cause to enforce this law.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u30	Dear President, honourable Members and Members, welcome! On the proposal for a law on the confiscation of property of illegal origin, the Government gave its opinion at the first reading or general presentation of the opinions held at the July meeting of the National Assembly. At that time, it supported the legitimate and legitimate objective pursued by the proposal of this law, namely, that no one may and cannot retain the assets obtained unlawfully in the event of the establishment of a suspicion of service of one of the catalogue-specific offences. The procedure for the confiscation of assets of illicit origin, as proposed by the applicants, is not a criminal or criminal procedure, but is a civil consequence of the fact that the assets were not obtained in a manner that would be legal and therefore not even result in the acquisition of property rights. It is precisely because the seizure process is not related to a criminal offence or criminal procedure, but against property, i.e. the so-called and rem proceedings, and since the offence by which the property was acquired was unlawfully at the time of acquisition, that is to say, we estimate that the introduction of the proposed regime will not interfere with the constitutionally guaranteed presumption of innocence provided for in Article 27 of the Constitution and also not with the right to private property referred to in Article 33 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia. At that starting point and in the fact that the proposal for a law in the current legislative procedure has been made by the competent committee and has already been subject to extensive amendments and that the provisions of the law have also been significantly improved on the basis of amendments resulting from comments or opinions from the Legislative and Legal Service, which have raised doubts about the constitutional compatibility of the proposed solutions and have also ensured the proportionality of the measures envisaged by the proposal, we believe that the proposal for a law will constitute a good tool in practice against the acquisition of property in an illegal manner. However, it is ultimately worth mentioning or pointing out that it is a new approach in the Slovenian legal order, that it is a fundamental change in the understanding of the possibility of assessing the origin of property law and that it is therefore good to maintain a certain degree of restraint and proportionality in determining the provisions by which these procedures are defined. We therefore assess that the law, as it is currently drafted, meets all these criteria, otherwise if amendments proposed by the SDS Group are adopted and my predecessor has already said that they interfere with these institutions too radically and too open constitutional issues to support them. Thank you very much.

Preparation of the second novel of the Penal Code [[KZ-1B]] is part of a major project that bypasses both the drafting of the new Penal Code and the Criminal Procedure Act, as well as the State Prosecutor's Office Act, which has already been adopted. This is enhanced by the introduction of specialised departments in the courts for the most difficult forms of economic crime. ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u120	Initially, of course, I can find that Mr Gorenak has clearly read the article in Del today, written by Dr Bele. The fact is that the Penal Code has only been in force for a few years, that is true. It is true, however, that the Penal Code was adopted in such a way that in the majority section, with little to do with the parable, somehow the three authors agreed with this criminal code, and all the other theorists and practitioners generally did not. The law was adopted in a very short period of time in a way that is not customary for the adoption of laws, but in particular for the adoption of criminal laws. We have had very serious proposals and very serious concerns expressed by the pool of the entire legal profession in order to draw up a new criminal code, precisely because it was created in such a situation and is a reflection of a certain state and time. As I said in the opening presentation, we did not follow the fact that a new criminal code was written on the nose and there was no secret that part of the profession was not impressed. They believe that it is legitimate that a complete or comprehensive solution to the criminal situation would be appropriate only in this form to adopt a new criminal code. If I return to this proposal of the novel that is before you today. This amendment was based on three sets or three factors. The first was that, as I have already said, we have been alerted by the majority view of the profession to the conceptual difference in this novel. The second part of the reasons for the change came from practice. Indeed, after we asked them about the application of this criminal code in practice, they pointed to more than ten, more than eighty concrete proposals that they had seen in their work up to that time. And that was the basis for changing the penal code. This has been the case for a third set of reasons, namely harmonisation with the acquis of the European Union and other binding international legal acts. Let me define myself to the specifics, as far as I have been able to detect them from Mr Gorenak's performance. The first to go to the detriment of a new crime — damage to public funds — is also in the direction of an article by Mr Belet, if it actually means amnesty. I cannot agree with this at all, neither I nor the experts who have prepared this law. This is because we have general crimes that sanction the abuse of power. It is true, however, that in practice, in recent years, we have shown ourselves, and I think that we will all agree on this, that the criminal proceedings in which the defendants would be convicted of such crimes were not quite enough. And that there is a public belief that the effect of the criminal court is not in line with the needs that appear in the public eye. Of course, we are well aware that in criminal law we must not follow what is perceived as a general need in the public. In doing so, it is important to be very careful because, in accordance with the principle of the rule of law, it is necessary to take account of the fact that only a court decides on liability for a crime in the procedure provided for. However, if we look at the statistics of the courts, we see that the conviction for such a crime is extremely small. However, if we look at the statistics of the Court of Auditors, which is the Supreme Auditor and the Ombudsman of Public Interest in the implementation of public finances, we see that success or result between what the Court of Auditors sees as an irregularity which it considers to be a suspect of a criminal service, and that it makes an appropriate complaint or a statement to the competent state prosecutor, and what eventually comes out of these indictments in the form of convictions is even worse. That is why the President of the Court of Auditors appealed to the Ministry of Justice, to the Council for Criminal Law to verify that the formulations of existing criminal offences are sufficiently clear to allow criminal proceedings. And after a careful analysis, which lasted for several years, we came up with the formulation of this crime, which is now under discussion. And this crime is nothing more than a special form of general crimes by abuse of powers. In other words, it is true that any crime can only be used from the moment it is adopted and established in the Code and that the law is applied for the future, not the reverse. This does not mean that those who have been in the past will be in the process of abuse of authority — but to calm you down — not very much — that these will be automatically forgiven. That doesn't mean that. This only means that from now on or tomorrow after this change takes effect, we will have another additional opportunity to prove guilt by other clearly defined signs. If there was evidence yesterday of a crime that existed yesterday, this crime, which is a special form of the general one, does not necessarily mean that those who were in criminal proceedings for general offences will have to be forgiven. Well, it's not an amnesty at all. It is a matter of ensuring more effective protection over spending through clearer definitions, clearer relations and a more stringent form of responsibility. With regard to extreme force and the exclusion of illegality and criminality. Although I am sorry that this debate was not on the Committee. I expected it to be, but the debate focused only on the crimes of copyright, and there was not much legal argument in the adoption process. What it is, these stages are behind us. With regard to the case referred to by Member Gorenak, I can only say that it is very difficult to imagine when non-payment of contributions could be defined as an extreme force. For the extreme force is the act that is done to deter the perpetrator from himself or another at the same time a danger that could not be prevented otherwise. If someone has operated in a company in such a way that they now have to resolve the financial situation, this is not in any way equivalent to the danger that the Penal Code refers to when it protects life and the body, so I don't know if all these arguments are really so convincing. Conditional conviction and extension of the possibility of a conditional conviction. It is possible to understand that some concerns may arise if society becomes less repressive. That's not my opinion. Of course, I do not want to underestimate, on the other hand, what we have already seen for more time, that there are many conditional convictions in Slovenia. However, the definitions under which conditions and the definition of conditions for the imposition of conditional judgments will not change this. That the conditional sentences are so high, more than 80% of all convictions, there is a large difference in the fact that the fines imposed are extremely small and in this relationship between the fine and the conditional conviction, if we are looking at a comparative Slovenia with other countries. That's where the sentence of fines prevails, and we have the saying of conditional sentences. But, as it is said, the fact that conditional convictions are handed down has very little to do with the conditions for conditional convictions. Today these conditions are very low, they are only easier crimes, and there are still so many. By amending the provisions on conditional conviction, we do not pursue the objective of more conditional sentences in Slovenia. No, it's something else. This is an instrument or a change resulting from the introduction of an instrument in criminal proceedings. This instrument is an agreement on guilt and punishment. We will discuss this further at the next point, but because there will be no debate there because there are no amendments, let me say very quickly what an agreement on the recognition of guilt and the punishment of criminal proceedings means. This is the possibility that the prosecutor, instead of processing an act in court for three years for which it is relatively clear that the accused recognises it and thereby causing costs to the State, will cause stress on the defendant's part and, in the end, may conclude an agreement at the beginning of the proceedings with the accused under certain strict conditions. An agreement defining what the accused is to be charged with, what the accused recognises and what criminal sanctions are provided for in this regard. This agreement will also be checked by the court, this is worth stressing, so it is not only a goal agreement between the two parties without judicial control. However, this judicial review is, of course, different from that in normal procedures. However, the benefits of such proceedings are also different from those of ordinary courts. Saving time, saving energy and thus more opportunities to use energy and time for more demanding procedures is what these processes bring. And it is precisely because, in those cases where an agreement is possible, such agreements would occur and so that the prosecutor negotiating with the suspect whether or not he would admit guilt or under what conditions or what kind of sanction is most appropriate for his action would have more room for manoeuvre that we have given these provisions on criminal sanctions, which only in those cases where recognition occurs, can be applied slightly. This is clear and understandable. On the other hand, with a view to achieving a greater number of fines at the expense of fewer conditional convictions, we also amend the provisions on the imposition of fines, which I do not know if they have been specifically mentioned in the presentation or in the position of the Member. These are important, too. With these provisions, we want to allow the courts to impose several fines on them because we find that there is a problem that is so markedly disproportionate between the fines imposed and the conditional sentences imposed in Slovenia, if we compare ourselves with other countries. With regard to the motions of amendments which go towards a criminal offence of infringement of material copyright. In my opening presentation, I said that I do not really understand this amendment. The committee reached a compromise, a consensus was actually reached, there were no votes against, namely, that the use of works of copyright for personal use is permitted, but that the exploitation of works of copyright that are essentially economic in nature is not allowed, and therefore I do not understand why this amendment, which is essentially the same as previously proposed, which has already been rejected on the Committee, is repeated. With regard to the offence of assault on the information system, where the deletion of Article 82 of the proposal is proposed in that part relating to unauthorised entry into the network, the amendment applicants explain that this could lead to too many people entering a foreign information system without delay. There's a lot of fear in here. We punish crimes and this crime is committed in concrete terms with intent. With an idea that means the unsub is aware that he is acting unlawfully and he wants to do so. So it's not an automatic or unwanted connection to a wireless network, but it's a consciously acquired username and password from someone you're not entitled to, and under its username and password, into an information system that you're not authorized to access. ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u142	Thank you very much, Mr. Chairman. Have a nice hello again! As has already been said, the amendment of the Criminal Procedure Act is part of a broader project for the reconstruction or reform of the criminal justice system, including the amendment of the Penal Code and the Law on the State Prosecutor's Office, as well as organizational changes in the work of the courts, in particular by the introduction of specialised departments. The proposed amendments and additions to the Criminal Procedure Act are, however, conditions to enable more efficient and faster criminal proceedings, while taking into account all the necessary constitutional guarantees. This is particularly true for the two new institutes we are introducing, these are a pre-trial claim and the possibility of an agreement between the prosecution and the defendant on the admission of guilt. The proposed gradual amendment of the law will also be a traveller later in the next phase, which is likely to be inevitable, this is the introduction of a new criminal procedure, which has been under way for years in the Republic of Slovenia. The Pre-Trial Order Institute is one of the central parts of this novel, which is before you today and by the introduction of this institute we are responding to the findings of criminal law theory and practice that the main treatment in criminal proceedings is often too long and too scattered, so it is not concentrated, and thus somehow hinders the effective conduct of criminal proceedings. The preliminary application is intended to clarify certain essential procedural issues before the opening of the main hearing. In particular, it is a matter of whether the defendant admits the guilt or not. This depends on the course of further criminal proceedings. If the accused pleads guilty in this pre-trial action, then a reduced procedure will be carried out, in which the court will examine only the circumstances which are relevant for the selection and assessment of criminal sanctions. However, if the defendant does not recognise the guilt, the task or function of the pre-trial claim will be to crystallize the subject matter of the dispute itself, that is to say, to clarify which facts are disputed between the parties, by which the evidence is demonstrated, which evidence is permitted or which concerns the admissibility, so that they are excluded in order to ensure the proper composition of the court, and that all those agreements which are otherwise procedural in nature are carried out for the course of the trial itself. When such crystallised substance of the dispute between the two parties, the main proceedings will later be more focused, in a shorter period of time, and will constitute a more effective criminal procedure. A very important novelty in our process law or process system is also the regulation of an agreement on the recognition of guilt. This is partly related to the imposition of a rebuttal claim, but in fact it is much more. This is about the fact that the charge and defence, i.e. the prosecutor and the defendant, who will also have to have an attorney, can agree that the defendant will be found guilty and in this agreement also determine the conditions under which he will confess the offence, which means that a criminal sanction will also be imposed, which the accused will agree with in this case. This practice is increasingly common in other countries close to us, but I would like to point out that this agreement is subject to judicial control as it will have to be confirmed by a judge or a court. The amendments are also subject to provisions relating to international cooperation. The aim is to unify and accelerate cooperation in criminal matters, and the existing international legal aid institutions, such as small international legal aid, enforcement of a foreign judicial decision, the withdrawal of persecution from another country and the extradition of a person, are changing. The harmonisation of the law is also necessary by aligning with the Penal Code, which re-regulations the treatment or safety measures of compulsory psychiatric treatment and protection in the health institution and at large. The harmonisation of the provisions of the Criminal Procedure Act is also necessary in order to satisfy the decisions of the Constitutional Court. With these proposed amendments, we comply with the three decisions of the Constitutional Court, while at the same time addressing certain shortcomings or uncertainties of the existing provisions. I would also like to say that, in the legislative process so far, we have removed the point that has raised the most doubts and debates, a point that concerns the protection of the privacy of communications and has been removed from the Committee with great support later. Thank you very much.

Entrepreneurship did not grow until the end of the 19th century, when Germany, as the first country, introduced an institution of limited liability, whose fundamental characteristic is the liability of the shareholder limited by the level of capital invested. So far, without the limited liability of the shareholders for the obligations of their companies, they could not even represent themselves. This is a fundamental principle of corporate law. The principle of the separation of the personality and property of society and of the associate established on all legal systems of the modern world. Such arrangements have one essential disadvantage. In economic practice, we often face the fact that shareholders abuse their societies to the detriment of the creditor. Therefore, economic law has developed very early mechanisms through which creditors can obtain payment of their claims from shareholders' assets. This institution is called the Presence of a Legal Person and we have it in Slovenia regulated by Article 8 of the Company Act. And watch out! This institute in Slovenia is practically not in use. While in such cases, for example in Germany, the market is regularly used to self-purify and exclude those who abuse market ferrels by means of the rule of law. In 1990, at a time of great financial non-discipline, Slovenia adopted the Financial Business Act and enacted the removal of the company from the Court Register without liquidation. The removal was due to administrative assumptions, such as the legal assumption that the company does not have assets if there was no traffic in one year in its transaction account. The effects of the erasure were horrendous. 17 000 and 80 companies have been wiped out and, according to the Civil Initiative, more than 70,000 citizens and citizens who are still paying the penalty disproportionately for their sins have been affected. These are not large fishes of Slovenian economic reality, but thousands and thousands of small entrepreneurs, whose business path did not come out because of lack of ingenuity, lack of orientation in a complex legal environment or simple resin. Even today, pensions are squandered, private property is seized, and in many cases children pay for their parents ’ obligations with their wages and also by giving up their dreams. After 12 years, he obviously had the time to correct this great legal and political error, according to the legal profession. You have before you a law which provides for a procedure whereby all proceedings relating to the removal of undertakings are suspended and the member has the opportunity to propose discharge of his obligations to the court. Two obstacles are laid down for the waiver of obligations – a legal conviction against the economy or conditions imposed to overlook the legal personality. The creditor may, by objecting and dismissing obligations, prevent the creditor from defending his or her interests if the court, in the process of determining the conditions for examining the legal personality, considers that the conditions for transparency are given and thus for the party's personal guarantee of the debts of the company to all creditors. The procedure may be completed in two ways. If the court objects, the debtor will continue to pay the creditor his or her liability, but if the liability is not proven, the debtor will be relieved of these obligations. Such a procedure, which uses the fully-known and existing institutions of our insolvency law, is an appropriate guarantee for the applicants that, even at the possible constitutional judgement, the Constitutional Court will establish the constitutionality of the proposed solutions. Such a procedure allows the bastard to remain punished, innocent, but the unfortunate debtor will be given the opportunity to end the calvary that makes normal life impossible. ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u150	Thank you very much, Mr. Chairman. Say good-bye again! The bill before you today is in its nature similar to the Financial Business Act, novel B, which also provided for the termination of the procedures already in place for the enforcement of claims against shareholders and provided for the waiver of obligations for the shareholders of deleted companies and the abolition of the personal liability of the shareholders of those companies which were deleted from the Court Register without liquidation. The government estimates that the same fate is very likely to be met by this bill. The fundamental solutions to this proposal, which relate to the cessation of the procedures already in place for the enforcement of claims against shareholders by exempting members of deleted companies, are likely not to go beyond the constitutional judgment. The proposed deletion of Article 496 of the current Financial Action Act, insolvency proceedings and compulsory termination, and its replacement by the rules of the new Act on the waiver of the obligations of the shareholders or the enforcement of the exercise of a lack of legal personality, do not meet the criteria laid down by the Constitutional Court in its 2007 decision. These solutions, without prejudice to the rights acquired or expected, interfere with these rights and also by arbitrarily amending the rules, the legislator violates the principle of equality against law and the protection of trust in law. Thus, the problem of deleted companies cannot be resolved in a constitutionally consistent manner with the retroactive effect, i.e. by the cessation of claims that occurred before the entry into force of this law which is before you today. On the inadmissibility of interference with acquired or expected property rights of creditors, the Constitutional Court has already stated, as stated. The waiver of the liability of the personally responsible shareholders of the deleted companies would only be possible if the State provided creditors with some financial compensation for lost claims, and in this case the issue of discrimination would be raised against those who have already paid such claims. In regulating any legal relationship, the drafter and the legislator must respect the decisions of the Constitutional Court. Not only because of the principle of legal certainty or the rule of law, but also because of the legislative economy. Because of the conceptual solutions brought by this law, which are based on the fact that the existing procedures are suspended and can also be definitively halted, and in the light of the problems of inequality of all concerned, we believe that these conceptual inconsistencies cannot be addressed even through amendments. As a result, the Government considers that this law cannot be considered further. This opinion of the Government is also based on an expert analysis by the Institute for Comparative Law at the Faculty of Law in Ljubljana. In the Government’s view, the regime for the removal of companies from the register without liquidation or unlimited liability of shareholders could be changed to a greater extent with the effect of going forward, i.e. for those companies that are yet to be deleted, not for those that have already been, and also by amending the Financial Action Act, insolvency procedures and forcible termination. However, there is no way to undo or correct these effects. The law which granted these rights was constitutionally consistent, the law was in force and therefore its effects cannot be eliminated. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u152	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Dear colleagues and colleagues! The proposed law aims to put an end to the judicial, administrative and enforcement proceedings initiated under Chapter 3 of the Financial Business Act and the Financial Transactions Act, insolvency proceedings and compulsory termination proceedings in which creditors of the deleted company claim against the shareholders of the deleted company which are not yet terminated on the date of the entry into force of the law. Having examined the provisions of the Law amending the Law on the Financial Operation of Companies of 2006, which, in a very similar manner as the proposal for this Act, provided for the cessation of fair, administrative and enforcement proceedings initiated by creditors of deleted companies against a shareholder of deleted companies, the Constitutional Court found that such a provision was contrary to the Constitution and repealed it. The Constitutional Court found that the legislator had no real and reasonable reason for failing to provide adequate protection for the creditors of companies deleted under the Financial Operations Act and concluded that the lifting of the unlimited solidarity of active shareholders was contrary to the Constitution. With regard to the termination of the proceedings, it has found that it is either an infringement of the acquired right of creditors or an infringement of the expected rights of creditors also protected by the Constitution. The Constitutional Court found that in a particular case there was no constitutionally permissible objective to justify such an intervention. The Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly notes that in the proposal for this law the legal situation is very similar to that of the Constitutional Court in the above-mentioned case. In this case, too, it would be the view of the Legislative and Legal Service that the rights acquired or expected by the creditors of the deleted companies would be acquired. However, the applicants state that the purpose of the proposed regulation is at least a partial mitigation of the consequences of the law which has laid down and which has imposed unlimited liability on the shareholders for the obligations of deleted companies. I agree and fully understand that this law, which we will adopt if we do so, is the cessation of the further enforcement of the law that causes injustice. It is necessary to know that this law has already caused some injustices, which will also need to be rectified, but I am personally in favour of this, as I also said on the Committee on Internal Policy, Public Administration and Justice, that this law is adopted, that it is on this path and that the will to eliminate injustices is demonstrated. In this context, it is worth stressing that the Directive does not contain only some guidelines which the State has taken into account at its own discretion, as has been heard at the meeting of the home working body, but sets out the objectives which the Member State had to achieve. Thus, only those things which must be recorded in accordance with the current Directive are mentioned in the draft law. Unfortunately, it does not regulate the distorted image of life or, rather, the life of the media in Slovenia. The legal conditions for a more appropriate and urgent enforcement of journalistic autonomy, which is understood by the Social Democrats as the participation of journalists in decision-making processes, do not create a more sustainable and stable source of public co-financing for media of particular importance. It is therefore not guaranteed what the Social Democrats believe should be, but these are not the objectives of this legal matter, which governs only a narrower part of the problem. Therefore, any comparison with the failed proposal for a media law aimed at establishing a comprehensive framework for the functioning of the media in our country makes no sense. Regardless of the modest ambitions of the marriage text, the Social Democrats welcome the provisions which, to a greater extent than ever, protect children and adolescents from harmful media content. They require the creation of product advertising rules at the time of the delivery of the children's programme and increase the demand for European audiovisual works and part of European independent producers. In the Parliamentary Group of Social Democrats, we understand the draft law on audiovisual media services as the last attempt by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia to avoid punishment and nothing more than that. As we are aware of what an additional few million burdens of the state budget would entail in these uncertain circumstances, we will support the proposal for a law. Thank you very much.

PERIOD OF THE DAY, THIS IS A DELAYMENT OF THE AMENDMENT OF THE AMENDMENT AND SUPPLEMENTATION OF THE MULTILATERAL VALUE LAW BY THE COMPLETE PROCEDURE The draft law was tabled by the Government. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the State Secretary at the Ministry of Finance, Mrs Mateja Vraničar.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u190	Mr President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The proposal for a new law was drafted by the Government due to the need to harmonise the applicable law with the experiences and solutions of the first experimental valuation of properties carried out at the end of last year. It is known that in the last quarter of 2010 all real estate owners were notified of the experimentally calculated value of their real estate determined by the mass valuation system. The owners received a million 98 thousand 349 notifications on the experimental calculation of the value of about 6 million immovables. By 25 January this year, owners made comments on some 550 000 properties, representing 9% of all valued properties. Of these, one third of the comments were related to value, 20 % to ownership and the other were related to real estate data. With regard to valuation, the majority of comments related to the valuation of agricultural and forestry land, more than 60 per cent, and to housing properties of approximately 15 per cent. The general evaluation and the experimental calculation of the value showed the need for certain changes to the law itself. It has been shown that it is not possible to uniquely bind models to types of real estate, but that it is often necessary to evaluate one property consisting of different types of use, such as combinations of business and residential use, to take into account several different models that need to be linked to the type of use and not to the type of real estate. In order to rationalise the experimental calculation process, the amendment of Article 10 proposes to delete the obligation to develop model proposals across municipalities, as these will take a look at the geodetic administration. It is also proposed that, for property owned by the municipality or by the State, a notice of attribution of value should not be sent to the owner, but that the owner should be given an electronic insight into the results of the experimental calculation. It is proposed that, in the process of determining the general market value of a particular real estate, it does not respond to the comments submitted to the individual owner who submits the reply, but after the final examination of the comments on the experimental calculation and final determination of the valuation models, it shall be sent back to all owners. The final calculation notice can no longer be contested and the value can only be affected by the change in real estate data in the real estate register. In carrying out the general evaluation, it has been shown that the basis for recording the price and rent data in the real estate market records does not permit systematic acquisition of the prices of new buildings. Even more flawed, however, are registered rents. This deficiency is addressed by an amendment to Article 22 of the Law. However, the amendments to Article 25 make it more clear that customer data are not public. All data on which access may be refused in accordance with public information rules shall also be excluded from the public view, otherwise the register of the real estate market shall be public. Your Honor! With regard to this regulation, the Government shall endeavour to implement it as soon as possible, so that the final value of the property can be determined not later than 1 January of the following year, when the data are urgently needed for the entry into force of two social security laws, namely the Law on the Enforcement of Rights from Public Funds and the Law on Social Security Benefits. Finally, the mass valuation of real estate should be completed as soon as possible also as a result of the provisions of the Law on the Placing of Spatial Arrangements of State Interest into a space which refers to the values of real estates identified in the mass valuation system in the assessment methodology for individual real estates. All this information or information points to the broader importance of mass evaluation, such as the tax on which the highest number of speeches were made in the public domain, and we suggest that you support the law in both the second and the third readings. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u191	Thank you very much. The draft law was treated by the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy as a parent labour force. I give the floor to President Bogdan Chappich to explain the views of the committee.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u192	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Nice greeting, Secretary of State. Colleagues and colleagues! The Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy is held at its 32nd session. Ten. In 2011, the Government submitted a proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the Mass Valuation of Real Estate to the National Assembly for consideration and adoption following a reduced procedure. The College of the President of the National Assembly shall be held on 9. 9. 2011 decided that the proposed law should be dealt with in a simplified procedure. The Committee was given an opinion by the Legal and Legislative Service, which made comments on the provisions of Articles 2, 3, 4, 7 and 9 of the proposal. The comments are included in the Committee's proposals for amendments prepared by the Ministry of Finance, which were made by the Committee in the context of additional work materials, together with the amendments tabled by the Zares Group to Articles 7 and 9 and by the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group of Ambassadors to Articles 1, 2, 9 and 10 of the text. The Committee also received opinions from the Municipality of Brežica, the Municipality of Lithuania and the Municipality of Maribor. It follows from the introduction of the explanation of the articles and the newly introduced solutions presented at the meeting of the Committee by the State Secretary from the Ministry of Finance that the text is adapted to the results of the practice shown on the basis of the implementation of the first experimental valuation of real estate. This is in the context of the solutions presented in the first place to introduce precision in the determination of valuation models, taking into account that individual valuation is not limited to the use of one model, as it includes a certain property with different uses with a parallel purpose of valuation and simplification of the evaluation process itself. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service further stressed that the comments in the proposals of the amendments of the Home Committee were consistently taken into account. At the time of consideration of the individual articles, the debate focused on the question of the timeliness of the simultaneous treatment of the law concerned by the tax law laid down by the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party, or on the amendment of the SDS Group to Article 1, with the emphasis on the solution that private-owned property is not subject to taxation of public economic infrastructure in the management of municipalities. However, according to the stated position of the workings of the SD Group, which did not support the amendments, the basic text of the proposal was already adopted, unrelated to the Real Estate Tax Act, mainly because the law in question provides for a basic review of the real estate base as a basis for determining the price of real estate in accordance with actual market developments. In the opinion of the Government, which has not expressed its support to the amendment, the answer to the question is whether individual types of property, either due to the presence of public economic infrastructure or other, will be less taxed or even exempt from tax in the exclusive domain of the tax law. ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u194	Thank you for the floor, Mr President. Before the beginning of the mass valuation of real estate, real real estate data had to be obtained in Slovenia. The purpose of the real estate market register is to systematically record and ensure the availability of data on real estate market transactions. The publicly available data on the real estate transaction prices and rentals realised in each market economy have a significant impact on the equal information of all active and potential players in the real estate market. This contributes to greater market transparency and to the equalisation of competitive conditions and a higher level of competition on the market. The main reasons for the adoption of the proposed novelty are the need to clearly determine how valuation models are based on individual real estate, the need to systemically regulate the recording of the real estate market, whose transactions are subject to value added tax, the need to regulate the system, the recording of rental contracts for buildings and their parts, and the need to regulate the mass charging of costs, additional expenditure and mass property valuation services. The problem with this Article is that the Geodetic Authority keeps a record of actual land use. What does that mean? This means that the Geodetic Authority can carry out a real-use real-estate valuation, e.g. a residential building on land which, by purpose, can be categorised as an agricultural or forest land where construction is not possible. This means that the state has thus evaluated black construction. The last real estate actually reaches a value of nothing on the market because it is a black building, and therefore the valuation of this property should be carried out only for the land without the building. It is clear from this that the Geodetic Authority does not link actual land use to intended use according to plan, which today poses a major problem. In the novel, we see the following lack of public information. In cases where the identification marks were public, it could also be possible to disclose from these codes information on the parties, which are not public under the law in force and which are contrary to the Public Information Access Act. In the Slovenian National Party, we believe that the amendment of the law will not achieve its objectives. It is only a virtual novelty that is supposed to make it feel that there are significant developments in the area of real estate valuation, which will ultimately contribute to real property values.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u196	Thank you very much, Mr President, for the floor. Good-bye to the Secretary of State and to you, colleagues and colleagues! And all due respect is also worth what a colleague from the Slovenian National Party said before me. There are always, and everywhere, shortcomings, reserves, or specific cases that need to be rectified and remedied. That basic law entered into force at the end of May 2006 and gave jurisdiction over the implementation of the system to the Ministry of the Environment and Space on the basis of an analysis of the behaviour of the real estate market, using data on real estate obtained from the geodetic inventory of real estate in 2009-2010. Therefore, in the context of the 2010 General Evaluation, the competent service produced valuation models for all types of real estate and in the autumn of 2010 in accordance with the procedure laid down by law, following the verification of model proposals with municipalities, carried out the first experimental calculation of the general market value for all real estates recorded in the Real Estate Register. However, in these general valuation procedures and in the course of the development of the value-adding procedure, it has been estimated by the competent services that in fact, in many cases the general market value of each property cannot be determined by a single valuation model, since real estates are often made up of parts which have a different purpose and, in particular, a different actual use. In such cases, several models had to be used for evaluation. Therefore, it also sought and found a solution whereby valuation models were used for individual parts of the property, for example, separately for the land and separately for the building, and the value of the property was determined as the sum of the value of the individual parts. The procedure proved to be more appropriate, at least for most cases. In order to regulate the appropriate legal basis for its implementation, it is necessary to amend certain provisions of the law. The main reasons for the proposed amendment to the Law on the Mass Valuation of Real Estate are as follows. The need to clearly determine how valuation models can be used in relation to individual real estate or whether multiple models can be used for one property. Furthermore, of course, consideration should be given to and the need for a system-wide regulation of the recording of the real estate market, the transactions of which are subject to the value added tax. In practice, it has also proved necessary to regulate the system of recording rental conditions either for the whole building or for individual parts of buildings and, of course, life also requires the need to regulate the possibility of charging costs, additional products and mass property valuation services. But everyday life also shows that the model of mass valuation of real estates is fundamentally correct, and many minor shortcomings, details that will need to be regulated in another way during life, in order for the system to act as a logical, coherent and reasonable whole. It is also important that the changes are not as much a policy as a profession and that the reform of this law has nothing to do with the new taxes. First of all, it is necessary to keep an accurate record and only later can it be possible to deal with further procedures or the use of these records for other, mainly tax purposes. The proposed amendment of the law will be supported in the Liberal Democracy Club of Slovenia.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u210	Thank you, Mr. President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! The aim of the bill before us is to partially transpose the so-called Omnibus Directive into the Slovenian legal order, in part, in the work relating to amendments to the Financial Conglomerates Directive. Although the Omnibus Directive applies substantively to the area of competence of the European supervisory authorities. The Directive, and thus this law by transposing the Directive, removes certain shortcomings identified in the recent financial crisis, in particular from the point of view of insufficient financial control. The supervisory models at national level are lagging behind the financial globalisation and interconnection of European financial markets where more and more financial institutions operate across borders. There have also been shortcomings in cooperation, coordination, consistent application of European Union law and mutual trust between national competent authorities. The main substantive solutions to the bill can be summarized into the next 5 points. First time. The new role of the Joint Committee of European Supervisory Authorities shall be defined. The supervisory authorities of the Republic of Slovenia, which exercise control over persons in the financial conglomerate, will have to cooperate with them and communicate to them immediately all the information necessary for the performance of their duties. The supervisory authority will also have to follow the guidelines of the European Authority before deciding on the equivalence of controls. With regard to the Joint Committee, it is also worth mentioning that the coordinator will have to inform him of the findings of the financial conglomerate and the appointment of the coordinator. Another time. Mediation of the relevant European Supervisory Authority will be possible when national supervisory authorities do not agree on the equivalence of supervision of superiors established in a third country. Third time. Better information will be provided. The exchange of information will also take place between the supervisory authorities and the European Systemic Risk Board, as will the ESA, a common term for the three European Supervisory Authorities, the right to access all information on supervised and uncontrolled persons from the financial conglomerate that could be crucial for complementary supervision. Fourth time. Capital and risk management procedures will also have to include appropriate development arrangements and, if necessary, the development of recovery plans and the resolution of individual institutions. Fifth. The supervisory authorities of the Republic of Slovenia will have to take into account the guidelines, standards and other recommendations of the Joint Committee in order to bring supervisory methodologies and practices closer together. It takes into account all of the above, the Government proposes to the National Assembly that the proposal be supported by law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u220	Thank you for the floor, Mr President. Dear Secretary of State, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! The current law adopted in 2006 was designed to complement supervision in terms of managing and reducing risks in relation to the operation of supervised persons in the financial conglomerate, thus also ensuring greater stability of the financial system. The financial crisis in 2007 and 2008 revealed significant weaknesses in financial supervision in individual cases, as well as in relation to the overall financial system. There have been shortcomings in cooperation, coordination, consistent application of Union law and trust between national competent authorities. Supervisory models at national level are lagging behind the financial globalisation and interconnection of European financial markets where several financial institutions operate across borders. Therefore, on the basis of the reactions of the European Parliament, the Commission and the Council, these findings at the level of Union legal acts have led to some changes needed to strengthen the supervisory framework for reducing the risk and severity of future financial crises. The aim of this proposal is to bring our acquis into line with the acquis of the European Union and to implement the principle of improving cooperation between national supervisory authorities and bodies of the European Union in the field of financial systems. The proposal of this law sets out a new role for the Joint Committee, whereby the Slovenian supervisory authorities, namely the Bank of Slovenia, the Securities Market Agency and the Insurance Supervisory Agency, will have to cooperate with the supervisory authorities of the financial conglomerate. Where the supervisory authorities of several Member States do not agree on the equivalence of supervision of superiors established in a third country, the possibility of mediation of the relevant European supervisory authority shall be ensured. Rules for the exchange of information between supervisory authorities and the European Systemic Risk Board are laid down. Capital and risk management procedures are being redefined. National supervisory authorities should take into account the guidelines, standards and other recommendations of the Joint Committee in order to bring supervisory methodologies and practices together. The DeSUS Group supports the bill's proposal because we believe that additional and improved controls are needed for the stability of the financial market. We believe that by increasing supervision we can achieve greater transparency and security in the functioning of the Slovenian financial market and thus the European financial space. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u224	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen of the House! The proposal for a new law that you are deciding today is one of the measures to restart the economy. With regard to the support of the economy at the time of the economic and financial crisis, it has been shown that State guarantees should also be eligible for loans for revolving assets, as some companies have completed their investment cycle before or during the economic crisis and are now faced with a lack of liquidity to finance operating assets. However, some sectors are so affected by the economic crisis that they cannot provide the necessary start-up assets for the financing of activities themselves. The second reason why we propose the law under consideration is that business partners, in particular foreign companies, require their increased capital adequacy to be included in the supply chain. Business analysis shows that individual companies are sub-capitalised. The incentive for financial consolidation could also be a State guarantee for loans intended to finance operating assets. The State's return on assets should therefore also respect this condition. On the basis of that amendment, the law includes the following key amendments. Loans can be allocated not only for investments, which allows the existing law, but also for the financing of operating assets, by dividing the total legal quota of one billion euros into 750 million for investments and 250 million for operating assets. The guarantee may cover 60% of the principal’s assets and continue to cover 75% of the principal’s investments. All guarantees are subject to the condition that the status of all guaranteed loans by law in a particular company must not exceed EUR 20 million; for the assets, the amount of each loan shall be limited to a maximum of 5 % of the revenues in the business year and EUR 750 000. Due to the special risk of revolving guarantees, the law introduces two additional guardians. The financial supervision and validation of all financial transactions during the duration of the project shall be carried out by the Authorising Authority. He will report to the Ministry of Finance and the SID Bank once a month. It will be appointed on the basis of a call for tender and will have to meet a reasonable level playing field as a bankruptcy manager. Its tasks and prizes will be further regulated by a tripartite contract and the beneficiary will pay the prize. The other protector is the requirement that members of the management bodies of beneficiaries or owners, as added by the amendment of the parent working body, must give their guarantee of at least 50% in order to secure the guarantee of the Republic of Slovenia. The purpose of this counter-guarantee is primarily preventive. Companies with less than 35% of the capital share of the balance sheet will have to submit a plan to increase the share of the capital in order to obtain a guarantee for the return on assets, i.e. for guarantees alone. If this plan is not implemented, the company no longer has the right to receive further guarantees under law. Due to supervision, the guarantee can only be issued for bank and savings banks with the permission of the Bank of Slovenia. As already mentioned in the introduction, the Government considers that the expanded guarantees offered by the proposed novelties would make an important contribution to the relaunching of the economy. In addition, additional cauldrons serve to ensure that the guarantees are designed for projects with a high degree of probability of success. In the light of all the above, we propose that the National Assembly adopt a legal amendment. Thank you very much.

At its 32nd meeting, on 5 October 2011, the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy considered the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Guarantees of the Republic of Slovenia for the Financing of Investments of Companies submitted to the National Assembly by the Government. The Committee was given an opinion by the Legal and Legislative Service, which examined the law submitted in terms of compliance with the Constitution and the legal system and from a legislative and technical point of view. The Service made specific comments on the provisions of Articles 1, 3, 4, 5 and 7 of the Law and on the transitional provisions. The comments of the Legal and Legislative Service concerned in particular the definition of first-class commercial banks and savings banks in which credits granted by the Republic of Slovenia were leased, in view of the addition of the condition that these banks were authorised by the Bank of Slovenia to provide banking services. The service has also been defined until the fourth paragraph of Article 4 concerning the clarity of the provisions defining the position of the authorising officer. The Committee also received comments from the Association of Banks of Slovenia regarding the determination of the market price as a condition that, in accordance with EU legislation, it is not possible to implement the scheme of law in State aid. Furthermore, the comments concerned the exclusion of the debtor established outside Slovenia and the additions of the article to the possibility of securing a loan with a derogation from the claims on debtors of different status forms abroad, provided that such a claim was secured under credit insurance. Qualified applicants have not tabled amendments to the law submitted within the time limit. The Committee was provided as additional material with proposals for amendments to the Committee to Articles 1, 3, 4, 5 and 6 prepared by the Ministry of Finance. Representatives of the Ministry of Finance, the Legal and Legislative Service, the Association of Banks of Slovenia and the SID of the Bank attended the meetings of the Committee. In the introductory supplementary explanation of the articles, the Ministry of Finance stressed the purpose of the bill. It was pointed out that the existing Law on the Guarantees of the Republic of Slovenia for the Financing of Company Investments was valid for a good year. At the time of the implementation of the law, the need to supplement the law was demonstrated in particular this year. This is followed by the proposed resolution of the law that the State guarantee is also a revolving asset. On the basis of the proposal for a law, one billion euro of the guarantee is allocated to 750 million for investments and 250 million euros for turnover. On the basis of such a solution, the guarantee for loans for investments would continue to be 75 % of the principal of the individual loan and 60 % of the principal of the individual loan would be remunerated for the assets. As part of a further explanation of the opinion of the Legal and Legislative Service, her representative stressed that, under the Banking Act in the Republic of Slovenia, certain banks that did not obtain the permission of the Bank of Slovenia could operate and the proposed amendments to banks that did not have the permission of the Bank of Slovenia did not allow these banks to be included in the scheme. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service also drew attention to the clarity of the provisions of Article 4, paragraph 4, which defines the position of the authorising officer appointed by the Ministry of Finance. In the debate, the representatives of the Ministry of Finance explained that Article 1 proposed a solution requiring the permission of the Bank of Slovenia, because it would avoid the granting of guarantees to banks that the Bank of Slovenia does not have full control over. In the debate, the representative of the Association of Banks of Slovenia submitted support for the bill to extend the law's solutions to reverse assets, stressing that the last indent of Article 1(4) excludes debtors established outside Slovenia. In view of the number of exporters and the different status forms of debtors abroad, this limits the possibility of collateral with a derogation from claims and debtors abroad, unless the YPPES will also issue a credit assessment for those debtors. In the opinion of the Association of Banks of Slovenia, the addition of the article also allows the possibility to secure a credit with a derogation from the claims to debtors of different status forms abroad if such a claim is secured under credit insurance. The insurers of these loans shall assess the debtor's creditworthiness and determine the amount of the claim acceptable to the insurance and, in addition, this framework is suitable for the collateralisation of the credit. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on the articles of the bill and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the text of the amended bill, which is an integral part of this report and which you have received, is prepared.

At its 32nd meeting, on 5 October 2011, the Finance and Monetary Policy Committee considered the Proposal for a Decree supplementing the 2010 and 2011 Regulation on the sale of state-owned assets, which was proposed by the Government to the National Assembly under Article 11 of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Capital Investment Management Act of the Republic of Slovenia for consideration. No comments shall be made by the Legislative and Legal Service on the motion for a decree. Meetings of the Committee were attended by representatives of the Government or the Ministry of Finance and Legal Services. In its initial supplementary explanation, the State Secretary explained that the Government had already proposed a proposal to supplement the 2010 and 2011 Regulation on the sale of state and financial assets, but the National Assembly did not support a slightly broader proposal to supplement the decree when it prepared or submitted the 2011 State Budget Balance. The second conclusion, however, provides that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, as an interested party to Adria Airways, expresses the interest of acting in the financial and business restructuring process of Adria Airways and the procedures for seeking the negotiation and inclusion of the best strategic investor in Adria Airways. The current book value of the stock is 168 euros. As a result, the sale of the country's stake in SIJ and the use of the sales option would achieve at least 5 million and a half higher purchases than could be made at current market prices. In accordance with Rule 130 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee has identified itself to the amendments tabled and has adopted the amendment of Mr Alojs Potočnik to the chapter Presentation of individual capital investments and to Table A. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on the articles of the draft decree, including the Annex, which is an integral part of the decree, and adopted them. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended decision, which includes the amendments adopted, has been prepared pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended proposal for a resolution is an integral part of the report you have received.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u271	I give the floor to the representative of the Government for an introductory explanation of the opinion. Mr. Minister Roko Žarnic, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u272	Mr President, honourable Members, Members! The bill you are considering today refers, as we have already heard, to just one segment of the Housing Law, to the question of the so-called alternative privatisation of residential dwellings. Until now, only the tenants who had acquired housing rights before 1991 had the right to use their homes for an indefinite period of time. The proposed Act on Amendments and Supplements to the Housing Law extends this right from so-called alternative privatisation to all domestics, including those who have acquired only the right to temporary use in residential housing, i.e. for a certain period of time, that is, during the performance of the household function, since housing was originally treated as official housing. The bill gives all housewives who have come to their homes before the entry into force of the Housing Act in 1991 and who are still staying in these dwellings the right to exercise the prior right in front of all other beneficiaries, including the co-owners, or the right to purchase an apartment under the conditions laid down in Article 173 of the Housing Law. This Article of the Housing Law thus provides for the method of determining the administrative value of the apartment and the compensation received by the beneficiary if the apartment is emptied. Accordingly, in accordance with this second option, i.e. the emptying of the apartment, all housewives who would empty the house could apply a retirement allowance of 25% of the value of the dwelling in the Slovenian Compensation Company bonds and a cash payment of 36% of the Slovenian Compensation Company and 13% of the cash paid by the Ministry of Environment and Space. For one average household apartment, this means a cash payment of around EUR 27 000, while the law also gives the beneficiary the right to credit at the Republic of Slovenia Housing Fund to an average of EUR 78 000. The bill explicitly does not only address housewives in multi-housing buildings, which could also mean the exercise of rights for housewives in business buildings. This means an extension of rights, as the Legislative Service of the National Assembly also pointed out in its opinion. If the legal provisions were to be understood as widely as possible, this would not only mean 250 cases as was the original speech, but much more so as to increase financial obligations. While the proposal states in the paper that the law will have no financial implications for the budget, the government has in its opinion pointed out that, precisely because of the financial consequences that the law will bring, this as the Government cannot support. Let us stress that, unfortunately, neither in the 2012 budget nor in 2013, housing resources are provided at a level that would allow for the implementation of the legal tasks already adopted. Neither are the funds provided to the Republic of Slovenia’s Housing Fund for loans, nor are certain conditions for granting loans — interest rate, maturity of credit and so on, which will present an additional problem in the implementation of the law in practice. I hope that in the event of a change to the Housing Law, for which I believe a good purpose, the future government will find the means to comply with the law. Notwithstanding the legal solutions envisaged, I can stress what my predecessor has already said that since 2009, all users of residential dwellings also have peacocks up to a subsidy to pay market rent. In the light of the efforts of the Initiative Committee of Housewives, the Ministry of the Environment and Space has also prepared an amendment to the Regulation on the allocation of non-profit rental apartments, where housewives will be classified in one of the highest scoring categories, allowing them to acquire non-profit rental apartments more quickly. In this way, our ministry is also trying to contribute to the elimination of injustices. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u280	Thank you for your word. After more than 20 years of efforts by different groups, the proposed law aims at at least partially correcting the injustices that the State has suffered from former domesticists following the entry into force of the so-called Yazbinski Housing Law. All the initiatives so far that the amendment of the law would provide them with adequate housing status, and these were quite a few, have been unsuccessful. If the proposed law is adopted, former housewives will receive the same benefits as the law has granted to tenants in denationalised apartments. The Housing Law has enabled former tenants or holders of housing rights to purchase cheaply in social housing, which is 160 dwellings at the average price of the current 7 000 euros. However, the right to a cheap purchase was denied to the holders of housing rights in denationalised apartments and former tenants. Housing has been regarded by the law as a service apartment which is intended only for the duration of the management of the household tasks. The so-called housing units have become co-owners of one or more apartment buildings. According to the provisions of the current law, the user of a residential apartment who no longer carried out household work had to move out within a year and was not benefiting from any advantage, even though he ceased to do so for objective reasons and not for his own fault. However, if the user of such an apartment remained a tenant, the floor owners could charge him marketable, in practice mostly extremely high, rent. Later, with the amendment of the law, the legislator even unjustifiably divided the former housewives into two parts: those who had acquired housing rights before 1982, which he compared with tenants in denationalised apartments, and those who had lost all rights and with high market rent. Despite the efforts and calls of the International Rental Organisation, the proposals did not receive support for the amendment of the Housing Law, nor could the former coalition. The reason for the non-acceptance was probably the solution offered which offered the possibility of buying a spare apartment. The last attempt to enforce the rights of landlords is more modest and relates mainly to ensuring the relationship of former tenants. The law should protect the right to rent a household apartment in all cases, unless someone has stopped carrying out the duties of a janitor at his own request. The new proposed law is applicable to former holders of housing rights or the rights of temporary use of housing, i.e. those who, on the day of the entry into force of the Housing Law, lived as active tenants in these dwellings; for those who have ceased to operate housework before or after the entry into force of the Housing Law, or for reasons for which they were not themselves to blame and who have not been offered rent or other suitable housing. The law thus provides for a number of solutions, from a pre-emptive right to the right to claim the reimbursement of part of the compensation fund and from the State and to a state subsidy, in contrast to the difference between market and non-profit rent, of course, all under certain conditions. In the Slovenian National Party's Parliamentary Group, we believe that it is high time that the rights of house renters are resolved, so despite the fact that the proposed law does not solve the whole problem, we will vote in favour of adopting the proposed amendments. However, we hope that financial resources will be adequately reserved for the implementation of this law.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u286	Dear Members and Members, Honourable President, Honourable Minister!
