We are pleased that the contractor is now accelerating to build this part, but nevertheless the energy part of the power plant will be built earlier than the accumulation pool. Since we do not want this problem of unreliable and unstable financing to be repeated by other hydropower plants, namely the Brežice hydroelectric power plant and the Mokrice hydroelectric power plant, we propose a solution to ensure a stable source of financing for the continuation of the construction, in addition to the energy part financed by the so-called Climate Fund or the Climate Change Fund, which is also designed to finance renewable energy sources, in accordance with directives and Slovenian legislation. This is therefore a real resource for this project as well, so the basic solution of the bill is that, through the Climate Change Fund, which will be fully operational from 2013, and partly from 2012, we will also start to partially invest the remaining infrastructure at the Brežice hydroelectric power plant and the Mokrice hydroelectric power plant, which are in the planning phase. Moreover, the fundamental reason why the new law should also be said that the Court of Auditors has found certain irregularities, ambiguities in the existing law and that, of course, the proposal for a law has taken these findings into account and these shortcomings, inadequacies have been corrected. There have also been blockades in the implementation of the law on land for the construction of water and energy infrastructure, which have caused unnecessary congestion, and we are also going beyond these problems in this new bill. Of course, the existing law did not regulate the maintenance of facilities in the concession area and therefore again and in this law we also regulate this area, but it is also, of course, true that the 2000 law, according to the decade of application and in view of the changes in the other areas, will survive and therefore need comprehensive renovations. Dearly beloved, the construction of hydroelectric power plants at the bottom of the Sava is the central national project of this country's investment in renewable sources of electricity production. This is not only a Slovenian goal to increase the share of renewable resources, it is a European commitment. And if we do not achieve this, of course, we will also pay penalties, so it is appropriate to speed up all projects that are feasible in this country in the direction of exploiting the energy potential of the Slovenian rivers. I am pleased to see some acceleration in the construction of power plants in the Central Sava and therefore this law, this model of public private partnership, the partnership between the state and energy, can be a good model for creating other important energy-use projects. Both Save in the middle stream and Moore and other projects. That is why I believe that the current consideration of this law has shown great support for this law, and virtually all parliamentary groups support these solutions. I am glad that this is a good journey, that the objectives of this law, that the chain will be built up in 2016, will also be realized. At the time of this hearing, I would like to predict that, because there are no amendments tabled, after the second reading, we will table a harmonisation amendment resulting from the amendments adopted at the second reading and proposed and written on the basis of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u287	Thank you. The draft law was considered by the Committee on the Environment and Space as the working body of the mother. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to Mr Daniel Krivac, Vice-President of the Committee.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u288	Thank you, Madam President. Colleagues, colleagues, distinguished minister! The Committee on Environment and Space shall meet at its 35th regular session. 9. 2011 discussed the proposal for a law on the terms of the concession for the exploitation of the energy potential of the Lower Sava, which was sent to the ordinary legislative procedure by a group of Members and Members with the first-signed mags. Andrei Vissiak. The committee was co-chaired by proposals for legislation, representatives of the Ministry of Environment and Space, the Ministry of Economy, the Commission for Local Self-Government and Regional Development of the State Council, the local community and the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly. In addition to the basic material, the Committee was informed of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly, the opinion of the Government and the opinion of the Commission on local self-government and regional development of the State Council. The bill was sent to the municipal councils and mayors of the municipalities of Rateče, Sevnica, Krško and Brežice and did not provide a written opinion to the National Assembly until the due date. In the introductory explanation of the articles of the bill, the proposer's representative stressed that the main reason for the adoption of the proposed law was to ensure the smooth financing of the further construction of the hydroelectric power plant chain at the bottom of the Sava in the future. In view of the fact that construction has taken place so far, there have been some congestions that relate mainly to the financing of that part of the infrastructure financed from the State budget or from the institution's fund on behalf of the State. Therefore, another solution was sought to ensure the smooth financing of the remaining part of the hydroelectric power plant chain. The law thus introduces the possibility of financing part of the infrastructure from the Climate Change Fund, which will be fully operational in 2013, and will be established in the coming year. The Fund of the Institute is thus burdened by the further financing of the construction of part of the infrastructure at the bottom of the Sava hydropower plant and the funds are more targeted at other flood-prone areas. The second reason for the adoption of the law is related to the findings of the Court of Auditors of the Republic of Slovenia, which seeks to amend the existing law in relation to a clearer definition of the payment of concessions by concessionaires. Certain problems have also arisen with the law in force in the area of land acquisition and maintenance of water infrastructure facilities, and some provisions of the current law have also survived. The legislative and legal service shall have a general comment on the proposal for a law in respect of its right to return. The proposed law interferes with the existing contractual relationship between the concessionaire, i.e. the hydroelectric power plant on the Lower Sava, d.o.o., and the concessionaire, i.e. the Republic of Slovenia. As the bill interferes with the existing concessional relationship, the Legislative and Legal Service points out the problem of the so-called unfair retroactivity, which may make the concessionaire's position worse. At a meeting of the Committee, the representative of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia said that the Government was in principle in favour of the proposal for a law, but proposed certain amendments or additions, which he considered should also be taken into account in the law. In its opinion, the Commission supports the local self-government and regional development of the State Council, in particular by finding that the current sources of funding, as the applicant also points out, are by no means sufficient for the planned dynamics of the construction of the hydroelectric power plant chain on the lower Sava. The Commission underlines the importance of implementing this project also for the Slovenian economy, as most of these transactions are carried out by Slovenian companies, which is particularly positive for the development of the economy during this crisis. The representative of the Ministry of Economy said that the Ministry supported the adoption of this law. In the context of the second reading of the draft law in accordance with the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee held a debate and a vote on the individual articles of the bill. On the basis of the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service and the proposals made by the representatives of the proposers of the bill, the Committee considered the proposed amendments and took them as amendments to the Committee. After discussion on the individual articles of the draft law and after discussion and vote on the amendments of the Committee in accordance with Article 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted together on all the articles of the draft law and adopted them unanimously. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended draft law on the terms of the concession for the exploitation of the energy potential of the Lower Sava, which includes the amendments adopted by the Committee, has been corrected pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure. The Committee therefore proposes that the law be adopted in the National Assembly.

Andrew the Visians, as we have heard. This Act repeals the Public Act on the conditions of concession for the exploitation of the energy potential of the Lower Sava in 2004. At a more specific level, it points out that the assessment of the state of the law should include an analysis of the activities carried out so far and of the funds actually used, including a draft amendment to the financing plan for investments to finance water, state and local infrastructure at a cut-off date; that data on medium annual flows that are the basis for calculating the energy of the available rotational potential of water should be included in the law for the determination of the water refund; that the concessionaire's obligations should not be reduced, the amount of the concession payment should be deducted from the electricity produced in a given calendar year. The government draws attention to the financing issue, in particular to the planned financing of the remaining part of the power plant chain, HE Brežice and HE Mokrice, as it is not certain that this is a stable and sufficient part for such a large and important investment. That the Climate Change Fund is a dedicated fund set up to fund mitigation and adaptation measures. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia supports the proposed law and, of course, draws attention mainly to the question of the financial feasibility of such an important project as the completion of the construction of the hydroelectric power plant chain on the lower Sava. The Climate Change Fund is a dedicated fund set up to co-finance mitigation and adaptation measures. Directive 2009/29/EC explicitly provides that at least 50 % of the revenue from the sale of greenhouse gas emission allowances should be used for the purposes which this Directive also requires. Member States need to inform the European Commission of the use of funds every year, and I believe that this will be a very interesting challenge for the future government. Thank you.

It should be borne in mind that water is one of the most important renewable sources in us, which needs to be fully used to produce practically the purest electricity. Given the fact that flood protection against 100-year-old waters is also established through construction, it is therefore clear that the completion of the construction of energy facilities is crucial for both the Posav region and Slovenia as a whole. In the Slovenian People's Party we support the proposal for a law on the conditions for concessions for building the energy potential of the Lower Sava.

This interferes with the jurisdiction of the municipalities in view of the burden of compensation for the use of building land which cannot be charged from infrastructure facilities. ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u306	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. My colleagues and colleagues, nice evening! In 1996, the National Assembly adopted the Law on Property Transformation of the Lottery of Slovenia, which also defined the Foundation for the Financing of Disabled and Humanitarian Organizations with the most important function, which is the care of fair and rational distribution of funds from gambling between disabled and humanitarian organisations. Unfortunately, since 1997, since the adoption of the amendments and additions to the Law on Property Conversion of the Lottery of Slovenia, the practice has shown that this objective has not been achieved to a satisfactory extent. The changes that we are considering and adopting today have been made, and some individuals do not agree, in dialogue with the public concerned, with persons with disabilities and humanitarian organisations. Support for solutions was shown by YHD, Soček Society, Association of Disabled Communities, Silva Fund and the National Forum of Humanitarian Organisations, which brings together 92 humanitarian organisations. During the parliamentary process, even before the hearing on the working body, we also tried to obtain concrete proposals from the National Council of Disabled Organizations of Slovenia, but we did not get any constructive proposals other than sharp words and discredits. A key problem that arises in the practice of the FIHO Council is the conflict of interest both within the FIHO Council itself and between organisations that have the right to run for or are recipients of FIHO funds. In addition to other tasks, the FIHO Council also has the competence to determine the criteria and conditions for the use of the Foundation's funds for individual purposes and to allocate funds between users. Both the Court of Auditors and the Anti-Corruption Commission point out the shortcomings and traps of the current law. Many suggestions were made on how to remedy perceived irregularities. We say that the proposed solutions in the amendment of the law are following this very objective. The Court of Auditors issued two opinions, 2004 and 2009, and the audit reports do not praise the situation, but have been submitted with reservation. The highest core of the criticism was given to objective criteria for assessing and evaluating the applications to be used in the allocation of financial resources to users. Of these findings, it has been a long time since the last report for three years, and it is only now that he has seen the light of the draft methodology proposal, which should have been tested for the first time only four years after this report, that is to say in 2012. The Anti-Corruption Commission has found that there is an unacceptable duplication of the functions of persons acting in the FIHO Council in principle. 137. At the same time, the President of the Lottery Supervisory Board is the President of the National Social Protection Association, a member of the FIHO Council, the President of the Commission for Social Protection, work, health care and disabled persons in the State Council, the President of the Disabled Society, the Council of State, which all points to a number of interconnected, intertwined and dependent functions. It is "the specific circumstances in which private interest can influence or create the appearance of having an impact on impartial and objective performance", wrote the Anti-Corruption Commission. In addition, this Commission has found a number of irregularities and inconsistencies, suggesting the risk that some corrupt and other illegal acts may occur. The most questionable and controversial is that someone decides to distribute funds at the same time and is also a user. Therefore, in accordance with Article 47 of the Law on Integrity and Prevention of Corruption, the Commission also decided that FIHO should draw up an integrity plan and assess corruption risks. Conscious of all the above-mentioned facts, amendments to the Property Change Act of the Loteria Slovenije, we are first of all changing the composition of the FIHO Council, as the current method of appointment to the FIHO Council makes it possible for conflicts of interest. The number of members and members of the Council is decreasing, humanitarian and disability organisations will be equally represented, and by equal consideration of disability organisations, the members of the NSIOS, who are truly pooling the majority, are preventing the exclusion of minority disability organisations. In Article 2, we add restrictions on who cannot be a member of the Council of the Foundation because of the possibility of conflict of interest. I am sure that, on the basis of what has been said in the Chamber, there will be little doubt about supporting the proposed changes, but in the future, I would like to see that the legislative solutions that threaten public interest can be made by the National Assembly faster than in this case, since the warning of conflicts of interest, the untransparent operation of FIHO and the possible unintended use of resources has been much more pronounced in recent years. I invite you to uphold the law. Thank you.

Such arrangements will prevent any conflicts of interest or situation where the responsible person, disability or humanitarian organisation, as an investor, decides on the allocation of funds at the Foundation’s invitation to tender. However, the third amendment concerns a more transparent use of funds, as a rule the Foundation should not create surpluses if this occurs, and the financing should be used by law for the next calendar year, given that FIHO funds are strictly earmarked. The Committee's debate has shown that the opinion in the amendment of the law has been divided, but we believe that it is crucial that the government, the Commission for the Prevention of Corruption and representatives of humanitarian and also some disability organisations have expressed support for the amendment. In the context of the Committee's consideration, we have adopted some amendments that have significantly improved the content of the law. We believe that the amendment of the Law on Property Conversion of the Lottery of Slovenia will allow for more balanced and transparent financing of disability and humanitarian organisations with a view to achieving public interest, so we will support it. Thank you.

In order to comply with European legislation, the novelty requires disabled companies to achieve a higher percentage of the employment of disabled persons, i.e. 50 % if they wish to impose State aid exemptions on all employees. Disability companies fear that they will not be able to meet the new conditions, which means the loss of these funds or their assessment of possible dismissals, and have therefore proposed a transitional period of one year in which state aid for protected employment can be obtained as before. But we know the next thing. The Regulation in question governing State aid has been in force since 2008. ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u326	Thank you for the floor, Mr President. The new Act on Employment Rehabilitation and Employment of Persons with Disabilities is necessary in order to comply with European Union legislation and is complemented by certain other provisions which have been shown to require minor changes and additions during their application and evaluation. This will help to limit the effectiveness of the law and make it easier to apply it and also to simplify the process for users. Key changes in the proposed law are the definition of control over the use of the derogations for the introduction of mandatory audit, the use of derogations, the extension of the grounds for withdrawal of the status of the disabled undertaking or of the employment centre, more detailed arrangements in the process of enforcing financial incentives for employers. These procedures are already governed by the Regulation on the establishment of a quota for the employment of persons with disabilities, but this will now be regulated by the legal material and compulsory vocational education for professional workers providing employment rehabilitation services, members of rehabilitation commissions and professionals in disabled companies and employment centres, thereby raising the professional level of their work. The proposal also introduces changes in other financial incentives, such as the financing of job and resource adjustments and support for the employment of the most difficult persons with disabilities. It also raises the level of cash benefits for the duration of employment rehabilitation from 30% to 40% of the minimum wage to promote the inclusion of unemployed persons in these services. In 2009, a new network of job rehabilitation providers was adopted for the period 2010 to 2013, which envisions a thousand 700 persons per year who will be involved in employment rehabilitation services and will be posted to the Service by the Office of the Republic of Slovenia for Employment. According to data from the Slovenian Employment Institute, between March and October 2010 the monthly remuneration for employment rehabilitation was on average 247 persons with disabilities. Some of the proposed provisions in the law could radically affect the current situation of disabled companies and their employees. The monitoring of the implementation of the quota system for the employment of disabled persons over disabled companies and employment centres shall be tightened. In the Slovenian National Party, we welcome the compromise solution that an employer who does not meet the quota with the disabled and has a contract of business with a disabled company or employment centre may reduce the obligation to pay the contribution to promote employment of disabled persons by implementing the quota in place. The bill also allows the sanctioning of offenders to a significantly greater extent than has been the case so far. The employer shall, as an employee of a quota that does not meet the quota, pay, by including the last day of the month for the previous month, a contribution fund to promote the employment of persons with disabilities of 70% of the minimum wage for each disabled person to be employed in order to fulfil the quota prescribed. It will also focus on the acquisition and use of funds received by employers for the employment of persons with disabilities. We agree with the proposed innovations in the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian National Parties, so we will support the bill.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u330	Dear colleagues and colleagues, Madam Secretary of State with co-workers, nice greeting again! In the course of the examination of the Draft Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Employment Rehabilitation and Employment of Persons with Disabilities in the National Assembly, we have found that there are several reasons for the adoption of this law, both Members and Lawmakers from the Ministry of Labour, Family and Social Affairs. In their views, virtually all parliamentary groups stressed that the main reason for amending and supplementing the law is to comply with the rules of the European Union, in particular by a regulation which prescribes the State aid granted to employers for the purpose of hiring persons with disabilities and for the purposes for which such aid may be used. Other amendments to the law have also been made to the debate, which will make it possible to improve the feasibility and effectiveness of the law and some solutions that will contribute to the greater employability of persons with disabilities. The key dilemma that we are facing today, as is evident from the debates so far, is to what extent we will recognize our common interest, that is to support the law that aligns Slovenia's rule of law with the acquis of the European Union. It should be pointed out that the specifics for the operation of Slovenian disabled companies were such that our country would have the opportunity to present them at the time of the creation or adoption of the regulation. At that time, we had the opportunity to influence the functioning of the content of the provision, which, of course, has the greatest impact on the operation of disabled companies, this is Article 42. In short, it was already late in 2007 and 2008. We are aware that the implementation of the General Block Exemption Regulation in the field of State aid entails certain restrictions that will affect business operations, but we have already stressed in previous discussions that this Regulation has been in force since 2008, and that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia has started the process of coordination with the representatives of disabled enterprises in 2007 in keeping with this change. It ends in 2011 and we can only find that Slovenia is already quite late with appropriate legal adjustments. Therefore, for principle reasons, the Social Democrats cannot support the amendment to Article 35 of the bill tabled by the Group of Non-Aligned Members. With the support of the transitional period, the law would extend the situation of non-compliance for a further year with the part of the General Block Exemption Regulation, which lays down additional eligible costs for employers who employ disabled persons. However, it should be clarified that the Employment Rehabilitation and Employment Act does not prescribe a quota of 50 % of disabled persons to obtain or maintain the status of a disabled company, nor does it prescribe that disabled companies must provide for protection of employment. The condition of achieving 50 % of the quota for disabled persons shall apply only if the disabled companies purchase new premises or equipment with input contributions, as defined in the fourth indent of Article 42 of the Regulation. In short, the condition of achieving the quota of 50 % of the quota of disabled persons employed relates to the specific situation. The novelty of the Employment Rehabilitation Act, in addition to the urgent adjustments for disabled companies, also brings a number of good solutions, which is under-emphasis in the debates. In this way, state aid will be used to subsidise salaries and the remaining costs of hiring disabled persons, up to 75 % of the cost of working with a disabled person, the cost of adapting the premises, the cost of hiring staff for the time they use to assist persons with disabilities, and the cost of purchasing and adapting software and technology equipment. In our work, the Social Democrats are always committed to strengthening the rights of people with disabilities. In this respect, we believe that the amendment to the Employment Rehabilitation and Employment Act on disabled persons in the field of the business of disabled companies brings both limitations and new opportunities. We also believe that the legislation of the European Union must be implemented consistently in the Slovenian legal order, and that is why the Social Democrats will support the draft law in the third reading. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u332	Once again a lovely greeting, honored present! It also extends the grounds for the withdrawal of the status of a disabled company or employment centre. More specifically, it lays down procedures for the enforcement of financial incentives by employers, provides for compulsory vocational education for professional workers providing employment rehabilitation services, and brings changes to other financial incentives, such as the financing of workplace adjustment and support for the employment of the most difficult persons with disabilities. The law provides for an increase in the remuneration for the duration of the employment rehabilitation period from 30% to 40% of the minimum wage in order to promote the inclusion of unemployed persons in these services. The right to use co-ownership or joint property was linked to the farm and was transferred only with the farm from generation to generation. We have proposed a 75 per cent threshold for reaching consensus on the construction of forest fire infrastructure, the construction of agricultural and forest buildings, agrarian operations in accordance with the law governing agricultural land, the conclusion of leases or leases and the modification of the use of real estate. All the transactions listed exceed the so-called regular management, and the reduction in the percentage to reach consensus will at least ease the difficulties faced by agrarian communities in their operations and will remain in most of the problems until further notice. A new agrarian community law is needed to regulate this area to suit most members of the agrarian community and not just some. Why the new agrarian community law? For today we are talking about the Law on the Re-establishment of Agricultural Communities, as we do today in the title. Slovenia is an exception to its neighbours in the field of agrarian regulation. Both in Italy and Austria and in Germany and even in Croatia, agrarian communities have the status of a legal person and as such operate normally. Their assets were not divided into shares or privatized. It is true that, apart from Croatia, it has not been maintained, but here is the key fact that it was not shared and inherited on return. Thus, there is no problem in obtaining consensus on business decisions that do not go beyond anything. In general, the state of Slovenia would enjoy as few such exceptionalities as possible. ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u347	Thank you. For an introductory explanation of the Government's opinion, I give the word mag. Tanja Strniša, State Secretary, Ministry of Agriculture, Forestry and Food. Please, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u348	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear Members and Members! The purpose of the act on the re-establishment of agrarian communities and the return of their property and rights was, in particular, the return of property rights to members of agrarian communities and therefore does not have provisions on the management and disposal of land owned by or jointly owned by members of an agrarian community. Managing property in co-ownership or joint property is governed by the Real Law. It requires the consent of all co-owners for transactions that go beyond regular management, such as the availability of the whole thing, the determination of the use method and the determination of the usable land. The problems of members of agrarian communities are related to obtaining the consent of all co-owners to enter into legal transactions necessary for the management of property. In practice, therefore, in many cases the management of the assets of members of agrarian communities is either disabled or severely hampered. The Government therefore supports the intention of the proposers to address the problem. Let me remind you that we have already taken a step forward with the amendment of the Agricultural Lands Act in terms of easing the conditions for agreement under the Real Law when it comes to dealing with agricultural land processing. Thus, the consent of the co-owners, whose ideal shares represent more than half of the land area if the agricultural land is in co-ownership, or more than half of the owners' consent, if the agricultural land is in common ownership, is sufficient to determine the user of the agricultural land and the method of use of the land. The Group of Members proposed to amend the Law on the Re-establishment of the Agrarian Community in such a way that more than three quarters of the members of the agrarian community could agree on their co-ownership shares for all transactions exceeding regular management. The Government estimates that the conclusion of certain legal transactions that go beyond regular management, such as land sales, the establishment of real service or building rights, the placing of the whole thing under mortgage and the like, without the consent of all members of the agrarian community, could constitute an unacceptable intervention in the constitutional protection of the property rights of those who would not agree with such transactions. Therefore, the Government proposes that transactions that exceed regular management, with a consensus of more than three-quarters of the members, be subject to a tax ruling. These transactions include, in particular, the construction of forest and fire infrastructure, the construction of agricultural and forest buildings, agrarian operations, the conclusion of a lease and the modification of the use of real estate. According to the Government, such transactions do not constitute an unacceptable interference with ownership rights because they can be justified by public interest. The government estimates that, secondly, it is not possible to ensure the labelling of agricultural land management. In view of the above, the Government agrees or supports the text of the amended law, which includes amendments adopted by the Committee on Agriculture, Forestry and Food of the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia, as it also takes into account the opinion of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u353	Please proceed.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u354	The villagers thus became owners of large pastures and forests, which they regularly sold and evicted because they needed to survive. The right to use co-ownership property or joint property was linked to the farm and was transferred only with the farm from generation to generation. Most of the total land has been transferred to private property, but some have been preserved until the 20th century, namely in two forms of ownership: ownership was registered in the land book to an agrarian community and its members, indicating the ownership of members by name, house number and similar as the special co-ownership of members of the agrarian community; ownership was registered in the agricultural book without stating the ownership of members' shares as a specific common property of members of the agrarian community and is further regulated in the rules of the agrarian community. The neighbourhoods were mostly transformed into co-ownership communities and from the 19th century onwards into agrarian communities, which were abolished by the Agrarian Communities Act of 1947 and the Agrarian Communities Assets Act of 1965. According to some estimates, the 1947 law in Slovenia is expected to abolish about a thousand agrarian communities. For several decades the assets were state or social, and in 1994 they could be re-established under the Act on the Re-establishment of Agrarian Communities. Co-ownership and common property were therefore fragmented among individuals engaged in agriculture and operating on the land of the agrarian community and other non-agricultural activities. In particular, problems arise in obtaining co-owners' and joint owners' agreement on the management of agrarian property, as interests are between members of the agrarian community and co-owners or joint owners, and as a result, disputes and blockades arise in the management and, above all, in the acquisition of building rights and the rest. The essence of this law is to cut through the Gordy knots, which have many agrarian communities, the consequences of which are infested meadows, decaying barns, pastoral dwellings, and so on. Everyone will gain this law by allowing changes to move in management, which will also be reflected in the increase in the assets of agrarian communities. In no way does the law affect the property rights of members of agrarian communities and their inheritance. We will, of course, support the law at the Liberal Democracy Club.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u355	Thank you very much. The position of the Group of Social Democrats will be presented by Mr Janko Weber.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u356	Thank you for your word and welcome on behalf of the Group of Social Democrats! The new law on the re-establishment of agrarian communities, which is before us today, is the sixth that we are considering in the National Assembly. While we note that the novelty itself is short, we believe that it will allow members of the agrarian community a few more opportunities to manage businesses that go beyond what is called regular management. While we want more to be done in this area in the House of Members, the situation is relatively complex and cannot be easily cut off, as this should actually fall into the Law of Law and the Law of Succession, which are, by their very important laws and must be protected. On the other hand, these two laws are linked to the Law on the Re-establishment of Agricultural Communities and the result is counterproductive, we are facing a number of developmental problems, as many projects are being halted. Why is that? The property was returned by law in the form of co-proprietary property if the co-ownership shares, or in the form of joint property, were taken away during the maintenance, if the joint property was taken. The property of the former agrarian communities was returned in judicial proceedings in the Slovenian courts on the basis of the same legislation, but unfortunately it was not always the same as it was, and therefore there was an unanimity of case law. The property was returned to former members of the agrarian communities. If former members of the agrarian community are already deceased, it is necessary, under the rule of Article 8, paragraph 2, to carry out the succession procedure after the assets found. Co-ownership and common property were therefore fragmented among individuals engaged in agricultural activities and operating on the land of the agrarian community and other non-agricultural activities. As a result, the application of the Act on the Functioning of the Agrarian Community to the Succession Act resulted in the fact that the shares of the agrarian community’s property are inherited, even though they have never been or have not been told. However, compliance with the Real Law has resulted in the need to find the hundred percent consent of all members, including all those who have already inherited and need to be sought around the world, often without knowing whether they are still alive or who are even heirs. So from a developmental point of view, this is extremely difficult. The amendment of the law passed by this law will allow agrarian communities, if only luckily, but a few more opportunities will be made to implement all those procedures that are extremely necessary and necessary for them to be placed in space, especially infrastructure facilities which we need in Slovenia, especially when we are talking about the construction of railway links. Knowing that this law, however, will bring some improvements, we will support this law and we can also say that we are looking forward to a further serious debate, especially in the National Assembly, so that we can also identify ourselves around who may even be a member of the agrarian community, because it would be appropriate for members to be members who operate and live in the area of each agrarian community. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u357	Thank you very much. The position of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party will be presented by Mr Robert Hrovat.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u358	Thank you very much for your words, Mr. Vice President. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we are aware of the problem that is today being addressed by the proposal for a new law to regulate the restoration of the agrarian community and the return of their property and rights. The basic law was intended to restore property rights to members of the agrarian community, but it did not regulate land management and management. For this purpose, the Real Law Code applies, according to which each co-owner may have his or her own ideal share, and for all transactions exceeding the regular course of business, the consent of the owners or co-owners or group of owners is required. In practice, this means that in many cases a number of co-owners and joint owners of agrarian property management are virtually severely hampered or even disabled. Complementing the text of the law is intended to eliminate the total blocking of the ability to manage the common assets of members of the agrarian community. The law is intended to define more clearly a way of deciding on transactions that go beyond the framework of regular operations and to deal more effectively with cases where, due to the unknown name or the title of the heir to the deceased member of the agrarian community, it is not possible to manage the common property. However, it is an extremely interesting comment when we adopt the amendment of the law, which is being discussed today by the first president of the umbrella organisation, the Union of the Agrarian Community of Slovenia, Mr Rudi Šimac. He was responsible for the author of the proposal for a new Act establishing an agrarian community and returning their property and rights, which was adopted in 1993. A reduction of three quarters will, in his view, be more beneficial to a country that has major problems with its projects than to you. Of course, then we are talking about roads, power lines, pipelines and so on, of course when it comes to land ownership. We will not oppose this law in the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party. Thank you.

Such arrangements, however, are totally lame of the management of agrarian communities, as members of the agrarian community are not able to agree on repairs or fences, let alone consensus on the construction of public infrastructure through the territory of agrarian communities. Of course, this will not solve all the problems faced by agrarian communities and make them more operational, as it will be necessary to consider the preparation of a specific law that would choose legal personality for agrarian communities and establish a specific body for agrarian communities, as set out in Austrian legislation for example. Members of the Slovenian People's Party will therefore be supported by the proposed law.

ANOTHER PROPOSAL FOR AMENDMENTS AND SUPPLEMENTS OF THE AGRICULTURAL BOARD OF SLOVENIA LEGISLATION LEGISLATION LEGISLATION: The draft law has been tabled by Mr Aloise Posedel and Mr Villa Trofenik. With regard to this proposal, the Committee on Agriculture, Forestry and Food proposes to the National Assembly that it be decided that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the President of the Committee, Franc Bogovic.
ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u364	Allow me to present this committee report in this package of agricultural topics. The Committee on Agriculture, Forestry and Food considered the proposal for a law at its 35th meeting, on 5 October 2011. The Committee was given an opinion by the Legal and Legislative Service, which, in the context of general comments, points out that changes in the law affect the origins of the assembly organisation, since the obligation or voluntary membership constitutes one of the fundamental elements of the definition of the system of assembly. Such an important substantive change always requires a comprehensive assessment and consistent legislative implementation, which, in the view of the Legislative and Legal Service, would be easier and more appropriate instead of a novelty under the new law. In the following written opinion, the Legislative and Legal Service also made a number of specific comments. The Committee was also given an opinion by the Government, which does not support the bill, and is not supported by the Commission of the State Council for Agriculture, Forestry and Food. It is also opposed by the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia. At a meeting of the Committee, the Chairman of the Agricultural Forestry Chamber pointed out that the amendment to the law was undermining the current regulation in the area of agriculture and rural development. The optional membership of the Chamber would also no longer be representative and independent and would lose the public legal status which, as such, can represent the interests of agriculture, forestry and fisheries. In view of the Government's negative opinion and a number of comments from the Legal and Legislative Service, to which the proposers have not drafted the relevant amendments, I have asked the Committee to discuss and decide on the proposal for a decision that the proposal for a law is not suitable for further consideration. In the discussion on the proposed decision, most members expressed their support for this proposal, as other members of the Committee also considered the proposed solutions to be weak and incomplete and the possible adoption of the proposed amendment would undermine the current organisation of the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia. The members of the Committee also agreed with the opinion of the president of the Chamber in order to lose its representativeness with the optional membership of the Chamber and thereby to represent various interests in the field of agriculture and forestry. In particular, the Committee stressed that, before any decision on compulsory or optional membership, a broader public discussion of the members of the Chamber would be necessary to show whether or not the membership supports the existing organisation of the Chamber. If membership were to decide on optional membership, such a decision would require a comprehensive amendment to the applicable law. However, interference with the law in force requires serious consideration so that these changes do no more harm than good. However, the Committee pointed out to the Committee's rejection of the proposed amendment that some amendments to the assembly system were still necessary. Although an important step has been taken in the organisation of Slovenian agriculture and the Chamber has undoubtedly played an important role since its establishment in all these years, in the current situation, the number of farmers active in agriculture will decrease, which is reflected in the number of mandatory members, but a new consideration is needed on some issues, such as the level of the mandatory membership threshold, the tasks or mission of the Chamber, the electoral system and so on. With certain, newly thought-out solutions, the Chamber could be a more qualified representative of all the groups represented by the Chamber, following the example of the Austrian Agricultural Chamber, which has an organised and diversified assembly system. Following a discussion on the proposal for a decision, the Committee adopted the following decision by consensus, namely 8 for and by no one against: a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on the Agricultural Forestry Chamber of Slovenia is not suitable for further consideration.

The draft law in the first part provides that the tasks of the Director of the Agency until the appointment of the Director shall be carried out by the Director of the Office, thereby ensuring the simultaneous management of the Office and the performance of the tasks of the Director of the Agency. the Director of the Agency. Article 2 shall specify the start of the operation of the Agency in the event that the Agency would not be entered in the register by 1. 1. However, Article 3 provides that all the agencies of the Agency will also have to be set up before the Agency enters into the register of justice or its commencement. ParlaMint-SI_2011-10-18-SDZ5-Redna-32.ana.u382	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. Dear representatives of the government, colleagues and colleagues! The Committee on the Economy is at its 39th session, 28th session. 9. Considered the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the Prevention of Competition Restrictions. The Chair's College is at its 113th session. 9. He decided that the proposal for a law should be examined in a simplified manner. The Committee also received an opinion from the Legal and Legislative Service, which examined the law submitted in terms of compliance with the Constitution and the legal system and the technical and legislative aspects. Representatives of the Ministry of the Economy and the Legal and Legislative Service took part in the meeting of the Committee. In addition to the introductory explanation of the articles, the State Secretary at the Ministry of the Economy explained that the main objective of the proposal is to more precisely and transparently regulate the procedures and deadlines for the entry into force of the Public Competition Agency. In the process of setting up the Agency, the Ministry of the Economy has found that the current deadlines and methods and procedures do not allow for a definite transition from one status form to another. In the event of an uninterrupted transition from the office to the public agency, circumstances could arise so that the Republic of Slovenia does not operate or exists a body competent to prevent the restriction of competition. The draft law therefore provides that, in the case of up to 1. 1. 2012 would not meet all the necessary conditions for the start of the operation of the public agency, the Agency shall enter into the register of the court only when all the bodies of the Agency have been established and the Agency’s start of operation is linked to the entry into the register of the court. The bill also provides that the tasks v.d. The Director of the Office for Competition Protection shall, pending the appointment of the Director, ensure the simultaneous management of the Office and the performance of the tasks of v.d. the Director of the Agency. The discussion expressed the view that the proposed amendments, which the Government, while seeking to increase the efficiency and independence of the Office, lead to a threat to its competent work. In this regard, a dilemma was also expressed as to the relevance of the adoption of the proposed law. With regard to the change in the status form, it was expressed that this does not affect the financial dependence of the Office or the Agency, since the Government or Parliament shall determine the budget for both the Office and the Agency. With regard to Article 1 of the bill, the question was raised as to whether the Government classified the appointment of the perpetrator of the duties as its current business, highlighting the possibility that the Agency would be without the Director or the Acting Director. After the discussion was concluded, the Committee voted on and adopted all articles of the draft law in accordance with Article 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly. Since no amendments were adopted to the draft law at second reading, the Committee on the Economy proposes to the National Assembly that the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on the Prevention of Restrictions on Competition be adopted in the proposed text. Thank you very much.
